60.05. Design Review Design Principles, Standards and Guidelines
[ORD 4332; January 2005]
[ORD 4332, 01/01/2005]
60.05.05. Purpose.
The following design principles, standards and guidelines shall be met by new development, and redevelopment where
applicable, throughout the City. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
[ORD 4332, 01/01/2005; ORD 4584, 06/01/2012]
Effective on: 6/1/2012


60.05.10. Design Principles.
The following design principles are general statements to guide the development of the built environment, the
appearance of that development, and the effect of that development on the existing surroundings. The design guidelines
and standards implement these principles. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
1.    Building Design and Orientation. Design buildings that enhance the visual character of the community and take into
      account the surrounding neighborhoods, provide permanence, and create a sense of place. In Residential,
      Commercial and Multiple Use districts, design buildings that contribute to a safe, high quality pedestrian-oriented
      streetscape.
2.    Multiple Use District Building Orientation and Design. Locate buildings so they are conveniently and safely
      accessible from on-site and off-site walkways, sidewalks and streets, and so buildings near the edge of a right of way
      provide a high quality, pedestrian oriented streetscape, contribute to safety by offering "eyes on the street" and
      promote pedestrian safety and use. Provide a pedestrian-friendly environment through building and site design
      treatments that may vary in nature and degree depending on the character of the urban area, the characteristics of
      the street, and the type of use and development proposed. [ORD 4876; January 2026]
3.    Circulation and Parking Design. Provide integrated multi-modal circulation and parking improvements that are safe
      and convenient, connect to surrounding neighborhoods and streets, and serve the needs of development.
4.    Landscape, Open Space, and Natural Areas Design. Create landscape areas that contribute to the aesthetics of the
      community, conserve, protect, enhance or restore natural features and the natural environment, provide an
      attractive setting for buildings, and provide safe, interesting outdoor spaces for residents, customers, employees,
      and the community. Whenever possible, utilize native vegetative species which are disease and drought tolerant.
5.    Lighting Design. Provide exterior lighting for buildings, parking lots, pedestrian pathways, vehicular areas, pedestrian
      plazas, public open spaces to ensure public safety and convenience, and to minimize excessive illumination on
      environmentally sensitive areas, adjoining properties, and streets.
[ORD 4332, 01/01/2005; ORD 4584, 06/01/2012; ORD 4876, 01/20/2026]
Effective on: 1/20/2026


60.05.11. Applicability of Design Standards to Residential Uses.
[ORD 4822; June 2022] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
For residential development, the applicability of the Design Standards and Guidelines shall vary based on the following
standards.
1.    In RMA, RMB, and RMC, all allowed uses shall comply with Section 60.05.60, except Compact Detached Housing and
      Multi-dwelling structures. Compact Detached Housing and Multi-dwelling structures shall comply with Sections
      60.05.15 through 60.05.30 or Sections 60.05.35 through 60.05.50.
2.    In CM-RM, all allowed uses shall comply with Section 60.05.60, except Compact Detached Housing and Multi-
      dwelling structures.
      A.     Compact Detached Housing shall comply with Sections 60.05.15 through 60.05.30 or Sections 60.05.35
             through 60.05.50.
      B.     Multi-dwellings with 5 or 6 units shall comply with Section 60.05.65.
      C.     In no case shall 60.05.15 through 60.05.30 or Sections 60.05.35 through 60.05.50 apply to the development of
             small-scale commercial uses in the CM-RM zoning district, which shall instead comply with Section 60.05.60.
3.    In all other districts, all allowed uses except single-detached dwellings (including Manufactured Homes) shall comply
      with Sections 60.05.15 through 60.05.30 or Sections 60.05.35 through 60.05.50.
[ORD 4822, 06/30/2022; ORD 4859, 01/02/2025]
Effective on: 1/2/2025

Structures, Non-habitable Buildings and Other Structures.

permanent structures not considered buildings, certain Design Standards and Guidelines do not apply. Table
60.05.12 outlines the applicability of the Standards and Guidelines by structure type, size, visibility, proximity to a public
street, and function.

60.05.15. Building Design and Orientation Standards.
All standards apply in all zoning districts unless otherwise noted in Section 60.05.11 or within the standards below. [ORD
4859; January 2025]
1.    Building articulation and variety.
      A.     Multi-dwellings in all applicable Residential zones, and townhouses in the MR and CM-MR zones, shall be
             limited in length to 200 feet. [ORD 4542; June 2010] [ORD 4822; June 2022] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
      B.     Buildings that have any portion of any elevation visible from and within 200 feet of an adjacent public
             street shall have a minimum portion of the street-facing elevation(s) and the elevation(s) containing a primary
             building entrance or multiple tenant entrances devoted to Permanent Architectural Features designed to
             provide articulation and variety. These permanent features include, but are not limited to, windows, bays and
             offsetting walls that extend at least 18 inches, recessed entrances, doors, loading doors and bays, and changes
             in material types. Different shapes, sizes, and applications of the same material are not considered changes in
             material types. Changes in material types shall have a minimum dimension of two feet and minimum area of
             25 square feet. The percentage of the total square footage of elevation area is: [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD
             4857; October 2024] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
             1.    30 percent in applicable Residential zones, and all uses in Commercial and Multiple Use zones. [ORD
                   4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
             2.    50 percent in Commercial zones where glazing is less than 35 percent. [ORD 4859; January 2025] [ORD
                   4879; April 2026]
             3.    15 percent in Industrial zones. [ORD 4462; January 2008] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
             In Industrial zones, where the principal use of the building is manufacturing, assembly, fabricating, processing,
             packing, storage, wholesale or distribution activities, the above standards shall apply only to elevations visible
             from and within 100 feet of an adjacent public street, and elevations that include a primary building entrance
             or multiple tenant entrances. [ORD 4531; April 2010] [ORD 4659; July 2015]
      C.     The maximum spacing between Permanent Architectural Features (to meet the requirements of Section
             60.05.15.1.B), both vertically and horizontally, shall be no more than: [ORD 4782; April 2020] [ORD 4857;
             October 2024]
             1.    40 feet in applicable Residential zones, and all uses in Commercial and Multiple Use zones. [ORD 4584;
                   June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
             2.    60 feet in Industrial zones. [ORD 4859; January 2025]
             3.    15 feet in detached residential developments in Multiple Use zones for walls facing streets, common
                   greens, and shared courts. [ORD 4542; June 2010] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
      D.     In addition to the requirements of Section 60.05.15.1.B and C., detached and attached residential building
             elevations facing a street, common green or shared court shall not contain an undifferentiated wall
             area greater than 150 square feet within which a box with the minimum dimensions of 10 feet by 15 feet can
             fit. For the purposes of this standard, undifferentiated means lacking Permanent Architectural Features. [ORD
             4542; June 2010] [ORD 4857; October 2024]
2.    Roofs and parapets.
     A.    All roofs with a slope of less than 4:12 pitch shall be articulated with a parapet wall that must project vertically
           above the roof line at least 12 inches and incorporate either:
           1.   A datum line the length of the facade that is a minimum of 4 inches in depth and height or a minimum 2
                inches in depth and height if it incorporates a different material from the rest of the facade, or
           2.   Completely recess the top one or two floors a minimum of 6 feet. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859;
                January 2025] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     B.    On new buildings larger than 20,000 square feet in total floor area, roof areas with less than or equal to a 2:12
           slope shall incorporate at least one of the following:
           1.   All the roof area, exclusive of space dedicated to mechanical systems, vents, elevator enclosures, and
                skylights, shall utilize a roof with a Solar Reflectance Index of 78 or greater.
           2.   An Eco-Roof surface comprising a minimum of 30 percent of the total roof area or 1,000 square feet,
                whichever is larger.
           3.   A rooftop terrace comprising a minimum of 30 percent of the total roof area or 1,000 square feet
                whichever is larger. At least 30 percent of the space shall be vegetated and at least 70 percent shall be
                shaded (by vegetation or structures) at 3 p.m. June 21.
           4.   Rooftop solar (or other renewable energy source) production of at least 0.003 kilowatt per square foot of
                gross roof area. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
3.   Building entrances.
     A.    Primary entrances
           1.   Non-residential and mixed-use buildings with ground-floor non-residential use(s) shall provide Primary
                Entrances through one of the following methods
                a.   Provide a Primary Entrance for all non-residential tenant spaces; or
                b.   Provide a shared entrance for multiple non-residential tenant spaces; or
                c.   Provide a combination of primary entrances for each tenant space and shared entrances for multiple
                     non-residential tenant spaces.
           2.   Primary Entrances provided to meet 60.05.15.3.A.1 shall face the street and provide a direct pedestrian
                connection to the street. Primary Entrances facing parking lots do not count toward the Primary Entrance
                requirements of this section. Buildings on corner lots where neither street is a local street shall place a
                Primary Entrance door within 15 feet of the building corner. Corner lots where one street is a freeway or
                freeway ramp are exempt from this standard.
           3.   Primary Entrances shall include weather protection that is at least 6 feet wide and 4 feet deep through
                the use of arcades, roofs, canopies, porches, porticoes, recesses, structural awnings or a combination of
                those methods.
     B.    Shared entrances provided to meet 60.05.15.A.1 shall include one of the following features in the entry design
           to highlight the entry:
           1.   Corner entries within 8 feet of the longest street-facing façade and at an angle of up to 45 degrees from
                said street;
           2.   An entry recessed at least 36 inches from the surrounding wall plane that has a width of at least 8 feet;
           3.   Projecting entry a minimum of 8 feet wide and 36 inches from the surrounding wall plane; or
           4.   Entry courtyard a minimum of 40 square feet. The courtyard shall provide two of the following site
                elements:
                a.   20 square feet of landscaping, in ground or in planters.
                b.   Pedestrian-scale lighting.
                c.   Benches or seating that provide at least 10 linear feet of seating.
                d.   A water feature.
                e.   Other element as approved by the decision-making authority. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
4.   Exterior building materials.
     A.   For Permitted non-residential uses and Conditional Uses in applicable Residential zones and all uses in
          Commercial and Multiple Use zones (except detached residential uses fronting streets, common greens and
          shared courts), a maximum of 30 percent of each elevation that is entirely or partially visible from and within
          200 feet of a public street or a public park, public plaza or other public open space, and on elevations that
          include a primary building entrance or multiple tenant entrances, may be plain, smooth, unfinished concrete,
          concrete block, plywood and sheet pressboard. The remaining elevation area for all applicable uses in all
          applicable zones shall be architecturally treated. Appropriate methods of architectural treatment shall include,
          but are not limited to, scoring, changes in material texture, and the application of other finish materials such
          as wood, rock, brick or tile wall treatment. [ORD 4542; June 2010] [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4857; October
          2024] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
          This standard shall also apply to all uses in the Industrial zones, except for buildings containing manufacturing,
          fabricating, processing, packing, storage and wholesale and distribution facilities as a principal use of the site
          where this standard shall apply only to the primary elevation that is entirely or partially visible from and within
          200 feet of a public street or a public park, public plaza or other public open space. [ORD 4857; October 2024]
          [ORD 4531; April 2010]
     B.   For Permitted non-residential uses and Conditional Uses in applicable Residential zones and all uses in
          Commercial and Multiple Use zones, plain, smooth, exposed concrete and concrete block used as foundation
          material shall not be more than 3 feet above the finished grade level adjacent to the foundation wall, unless
          pigmented, textured, or both. In Industrial districts, foundations may extend up to 4 feet above the finished
          grade level. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]
5.   Roof-mounted equipment.
     A.   All roof-mounted equipment shall be screened from view from adjacent streets or adjacent properties in one
          of the following ways:
          1.   A parapet wall; or
          2.   A screen around the equipment that is made of a primary exterior finish material used on other portions
               of the building; or
          3.   Setback from the street-facing elevation such that it is not visible from the public street(s); or
          4.   Screened from view by another building. [ORD 4782; April 2020]
     B.   As shown in the diagram below, the vertical measuring distance for required screening shall be measured at
          5 feet above the finished or existing grade at the property line or public right-of-way abutting the development
          site's front yard setback for a distance of 100 lineal feet measured outward from the development site's front
          property line. Once the vertical measuring distance is established for the site's front yard, this same vertical
          measuring distance shall be applied to all sides of the development site's perimeter property lines. [ORD 4531;
          April 2010] [ORD 4782; April 2020] [ORD 4859; January 2025]




     C.   Solar panels, dishes/antennas, pipes, vents, and chimneys are exempt from this standard.
6.   Building location and orientation along streets in Commercial and Multiple Use zones. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
     A.   Excepting buildings in the CM-CS zone and buildings used for non-residential or multiple uses in the CM-HDR
          zone, buildings shall occupy a minimum percentage of a site frontage between the minimum setback and the
          maximum setback established in the zoning district’s site development standards or 16 feet if there is no
     maximum setback listed in the site development standards. Accessory structures, non-habitable buildings, and
     commercial storage structures that do not require building permits shall not be used to satisfy this standard.
     1.   At least 60 percent of the site’s Primary Frontage on streets or Primary Accessways shall be occupied by a
          building or buildings. The Primary Frontage shall be determined in order of importance as follows:
          a.   Street with Frequent Transit Service
          b.   Street with non-frequent transit service
          c.   Street with the highest classification, (excepting those with freeway or principal arterial functional
               classifications)
          d.   A Primary Accessway
     2.   35 percent of the abutting secondary public street frontage where a parcel abuts more than one public
          street or Primary Accessway. When a parcel abuts more than two public streets or Primary Accessway,
          the applicant shall designate the secondary frontage at time of initial development, and there shall be no
          frontage requirement on any remaining abutting public streets or Primary Accessways.
     3.   For multi-dwelling buildings with a central courtyard space that opens to a street or Primary Accessway,
          at least 75 percent of the site’s frontage on any street or Primary Accessway shall be occupied by
          buildings and the central courtyard.
     4.   For the purpose of calculating the percentage of site frontage occupied by a building the following maybe
          subtracted from the site frontage length:
          a.   The width of the widest part of the driveway’s public element for a maximum of one driveway; and
          b.   Areas determined to be unbuildable due to sight clearance and sight distance requirements in the
               Engineering Design Manual; and
          c.   Plazas up to 20 percent of the total public street frontage per site. To qualify, the plaza shall be
               placed next to or in front of a building that is set back no more than 40 feet from the right of way.
               When located between a building and a sidewalk, direct pedestrian access between the building and
               sidewalk must be provided to the abutting building; and
          d.   Natural Areas. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
B.   All buildings in the CM-CS zone and buildings used for non-residential or multiple uses in the CM-HDR zone
     shall occupy a minimum public street frontage as follows:
     1.   50 percent of the abutting Primary Frontage. When a parcel abuts more than one public street, the
          primary public street frontage shall be determined in the following descending order:
          a.   Property line abutting a designated Neighborhood Route street or the property line where any
               portion is across the street from a Public Park;
          b.   Property line abutting a designated Collector street; or
          c.   In the absence of a public street frontage meeting a. or b. above, the applicant shall designate the
               primary public street frontage at the time of initial development.
     2.   35 percent of the abutting secondary public street frontage where a parcel abuts more than one public
          street. When a parcel abuts more than two public streets, the applicant shall designate the secondary
          public street frontage at time of initial development, and there shall be no public street frontage
          requirement on any remaining abutting public streets.
     3.   The following shall be subtracted from the calculations of total primary and secondary public street
          frontages:
          a.   The width of the widest part of a driveway’s public element;
          b.   Areas determined to be unbuildable due to sight clearance and sight distance requirements in the
               Engineering Design Manual;
          c.   Existing Public Utility Easements;
          d.   Plazas, including Pedestrian Plazas, up to 20 percent of the total public street frontage per site;
          e.   Natural Areas; and
                f.   The width of the travel surface of trails, up to a maximum of 20 feet. This exemption does not apply
                     when any portion of a trail that travels parallel to any portion of a property line abutting a street.
                     [ORD 4879; April 2026]
           [ORD 4859; January 2025]​​
     C.    Vehicle parking and circulation areas within 10 feet of a street lot line shall be limited to no more than 35
           percent of the length of any street lot line. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     D.    Areas within 10 feet of any street lot line that are not occupied by a building or vehicle area shall be
           landscaped to the standards of Section 60.05.25 or hardscaped for pedestrian use. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
7.   Height Transition.
     A.    On the portion of the site less than or equal to 30 feet from the lot line of a lot zoned RMA, RMB, RMC, or a
           comparable Washington County zone as indicated in Table 1.5.2 of the Comprehensive Plan, the maximum
           building height shall be 60 feet or the maximum height permitted in the abutting residential district, whichever
           is greater. This standard does not apply when the property zoned RMA, RMB, or RMC, or a comparable
           Washington County zone is not vacant and contains no dwellings. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
8.   Ground floor elevations in Commercial and Multiple Use zones.
     A.    Ground-floor elevations entirely or partially visible from and within 200 feet of a public street, Primary
           Accessway, public park, public plaza or other public open space, and elevations that include a
           Primary Entrance or multiple tenant entrances, shall have the following minimum percentage of the ground-
           floor elevation area between 2 and 10 feet in height from finish grade permanently treated with windows or
           glass doorway openings. All glass used to meet these requirements shall have a Visible Transmittance (VT)
           value of 0.6 or greater. [ORD 4857; October 2024] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
           1.   Non-residential uses: 50 percent for Primary Frontages and 40 percent for non-primary frontages [ORD
                4879; April 2026]
           2.   Multi-Dwelling residential uses: 30 percent [ORD 4879; April 2026]
           3.   Townhouse residential uses 25 percent [ORD 4879; April 2026]
           4.   Primary public street frontages in CM-CS and CM-HDR: 50 percent. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
           5.   Secondary public street frontages in CM-CS and CM-HDR zoning districts: 35 percent. [ORD 4859; January
                2025] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     B.    Structured parking shall have a minimum of 30 percent wall openings that utilize decorative screening
           systems. All openings shall be no less than 3.5 feet from the top of the parking slab so vehicle headlights do
           not trespass beyond the building facade. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     C.    Buildings not used exclusively for residential use shall provide weather protection such as permanent awnings,
           canopies, overhangs, or architectural features. Such weather protection shall meet all the following standards:
           1.   Weather protection shall be provided:
                a.   For a minimum 50 percent of the length of Primary Frontage elevations and 35 percent on other
                     street-facing elevations, or
                b.   Over all ground-floor entries and windows.
           2.   Weather protection shall have a minimum depth of 4 feet that may be achieved through projections or
                recesses from the primary facade, or a combination thereof.
           3.   Weather protection shall be provided over the public right of way or hardscaped area within a pedestrian
                amenity space with a clear area at least 3 feet in depth between the building and the street.
           4.   Weather protection that extends over the public right of way shall comply with the Oregon Structural
                Specialty Code in effect at the time of application for projections or encroachments into the public right-
                of-way. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     D.    Buildings taller than 30 feet shall have a defined base on building elevations visible from and within 200 feet of
           a public street and on elevations that include a primary building entrance or multiple tenant entrances by
           using one of the following methods: [ORD 4879; April 2026]
          1.   Floor-to-floor height of the ground floor is a minimum of 3 feet taller than the average of the remaining
               floor-to-floor heights.
          2.   Ground-floor level is set back a minimum of 2 feet from the primary building wall for at least 70 percent
               of the building elevation length.
          3.   All floors above the ground-floor level are set back a minimum of 2 feet from the ground-floor level for at
               least 70 percent of the building elevation length.
          4.   A datum line is provided between the ground floor and second floor. The datum line shall be a minimum
               of 4 inches in depth and height. The datum line may be a minimum of 2 inches in depth and height if the
               primary exterior building material, excluding windows, changes between the first and second floor. The
               datum line may project or be recessed.
          [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
9.   Compact Detached Housing design. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
     A.   Primary building entrances and porches accessing a primary entrance shall face streets, common greens or
          shared courts and must meet the requirements of Section 60.05.15.3. Porches, if provided, shall have clear
          dimensions of at least 6 feet wide and 4 feet deep, and shall be covered by a roof. The primary entrance must
          be within 4 feet of grade, as measured as the average grade along the foundation of the longest wall of the
          building elevation containing the primary entrance or porch. [ORD 4576; January 2012] [ORD 4859; January
          2025]​
     B.   Elevations on residential units facing streets, common greens and shared courts shall have a minimum of 25
          percent of the elevation area on each floor permanently treated with windows.
     C.   Windows shall be vertical or square in proportion. Horizontal windows may be created when vertical windows
          or a mixture of vertical and other shaped windows are grouped together, or there is a row of clerestory
          windows across the top of the grouped windows.
     D.   [ORD 4576; January 2012] Alleys (including access easements) and shared courts are the preferred options to
          serve garages, and shall be provided on all lots except where topography, or other identified physical
          constraints preclude their use. Within a project, the majority of units shall have garages accessed from alleys
          or shared courts.
     E.   When parking is provided in a garage facing a street or shared court, the following standards must be met:
          1.   No more than 50 percent of the horizontal length of the ground floor elevation facing a street or shared
               court shall be an attached garage door entrance (i.e., garage doorway) or 12 feet long, whichever is
               greater. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
          2.   Garages shall be recessed at least 1.5 feet from the ground floor front of the dwelling. [ORD 4859;
               January 2025]​
          3.   There may be no more than two individual garage doors per dwelling unit.
          4.   The width of the driveway (at the back of the sidewalk) shall not exceed 12 feet for single lane and 16 feet
               for double lane driveways. [ORD 4576; January 2012]
          5.   A maximum of 50 percent of the units within a project shall have driveways 16 feet in width. No two lots
               of this configuration shall be adjacent to one another. [ORD 4576; January 2012] [ORD 4859; January
               2025]​
     F.   Garages that face a street or shared court shall contain one or more of the following design features. Garages
          shall not terminate the view into shared courts from a public street unless they contain two or more of the
          following design features:
          1.   Garage trellis or pergola extending at least 12 inches from the building face.
          2.   Windows on 15 percent of the garage door. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
          3.   Decorative hardware.
          4.   Natural wood finish.
          5.   A recess of at least 3 feet. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
          6.   Multiple materials finish or colors are used.
G.   Residential structures must have a roof that meets or exceeds the requirements of Section 60.05.15.2.
H.   There must be architectural detailing that varies from unit to unit. Architectural detailing includes but is not
     limited to the following list. Some design features include requirements that exceed a minimum standard
     found elsewhere in this code. Each dwelling shall utilize at least 5 of the following design features: [ORD 4859;
     January 2025]​
     1.   The use of different exterior siding materials. In general, materials should change on horizontal planes,
          not vertical planes. Types of siding materials include:
          a.   Horizontal lap siding, including simulated horizontal lap siding where the boards in the pattern are 6
               inches or less in width;
          b.   Vertical cedar siding;
          c.   Beveled siding,
          d.   Shingles
          e.   Stucco
          f.   Brick
          g.   Stone
          h.   Scored masonry
          i.   Changes in a combination of texture, pattern or color of a single material
     2.   A primary sloped roof that is no flatter than 6/12 and no steeper than 12/12. (Exceeds the requirements
          of Section 60.05.15.2.).
     3.   A tile or shake roof.
     4.   One or more dormers on the front elevation.
     5.   Three or more gables.
     6.   Elevations on residential units facing streets, common greens and shared courts having at least 40
          percent of the elevation area on each floor permanently treated with windows (Exceeds the
          requirements of Section 60.05.15.9.B.).
     7.   Window shutters on windows which face streets, common greens or shared courts.
     8.   Bay or bowed windows on the front elevation.
     9.   Trim marking roof lines, porches, windows and doors on all elevations. The trim must be at least 3.5
          inches wide. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
     10. Weather protection for primary building entrances that exceed the minimum area requirements of
         Section 60.05.15.3 by 25 percent. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
     11. Porches on the front elevation that have clear dimensions of at least eight feet wide and six feet deep,
         and are covered by a roof supported by structurally integral columns or brackets.
     12. Balcony on the same façade as the main entrance. The balcony must be at least 48 square feet and a
         minimum 8 feet wide and must be accessible from the interior living space of the house.
     13. An attached garage with a gable or hip roof, or with a second story above the garage.
     14. Building face or roof offsets (minimum 12 inch offset) on the front elevation.
     15. Permanent planter boxes of at least 25 square feet constructed as an extension of a front elevation or
         primary building entrance.
     16. A landscaped courtyard of at least 100 square feet located as a transition element between a primary
         building entrance and a street, common green or shared court. The landscaping within the courtyard shall
         exceed the minimum requirements of Section 60.05.25.4 by 15 percent. [ORD 4576; January 2012] [ORD
         4859; January 2025]​
     17. Other architectural or design elements that apply to at least 10 percent of the front elevation and result
         in visual interest, variety and beauty.
I.   Building elevations shall not be repeated across a street, shared court or common green from each other or on
     adjacent parcels. In these instances, elevations shall have at least 5 different design details as described in
             Section H. above. [ORD 4576; January 2012] [ORD 4782; April 2020]
      J.     The following minimum setbacks apply:
             1.    Front yard fronting common greens and shared courts: 3 feet [ORD 4879; April 2026]
             2.    Front yard fronting street: 5 feet to building, 3 feet to porch or stoop [ORD 4879; April 2026]
             3.    Side yard: 3 feet, or 8 feet if abutting a street [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
             4.    Rear yard: 0 feet [ORD 4879; April 2026]
             5.    Garage entrance setback from street: 18.5 feet [ORD 4879; April 2026]
             6.    No side, rear, or garage entrance setback is required from a lot line abutting an alley or shared court.
             [ORD 4576; January 2012]​
      K.     Minimum standards for alleys are:
             1.    Alley width: 24 feet between buildings at the ground floor, and a paved area at least 16 feet wide. [ORD
                   4879; April 2026]
             2.    An alley may be up to 150 feet long. The Facilities Review Committee has the discretion to review alley
                   length, with optional design requirements to allow for alleys to be longer than 150 feet. [ORD 4584; June
                   2012]
             [ORD 4576; January 2012]​
      L.     The side building setback on one side of a structure may be zero subject to the following:
             1.    The zero setback does not apply to the property line adjacent to a street or to the property line adjacent
                   to lots that are not part of the zero lot line project.
             2.    The minimum distance between all buildings in the zero lot line project must be equal to twice the
                   required side yard setback shown in Section J.
             [ORD 4576; January 2012]​
[ORD 4542; June 2010] [ORD 4857; October 2024]
[ORD 4332, 01/01/2005; ORD 4462, 01/10/2008; ORD 4531, 04/01/2010; ORD 4542, 06/17/2010; ORD 4576,
01/06/2012; ORD 4584, 06/01/2012; ORD 4659, 07/10/2015; ORD 4706, 05/19/2017; ORD 4758, 03/22/2019; ORD 4782,
04/17/2020; ORD 4822, 06/30/2022; ORD 4857, 10/03/2024; ORD 4859, 01/02/2025; ORD 4879, 04/02/2026]
Effective on: 4/2/2026


60.05.20. Circulation and Parking Design Standards.
All standards apply in all zoning districts unless otherwise noted in Section 60.05.11 or within the standards below. [ORD
4859; January 2025]​
1.    Connections to the public street system.
      A.     Pedestrian, bicycle, and motor vehicle connections shall be provided between the on-site circulation system
             and adjacent existing and planned streets as specified in Tables 6.1 through 6.6 and Figures 6.1 through 6.23
             of the Comprehensive Plan Transportation Element. For properties within the South Cooper Mountain
             Community Plan area, pedestrian, bicycle, and motor vehicle connections shall be provided between the on-
             site circulation system and adjacent existing and planned streets and trails as specified in Figure 10:
             Community Plan Street Framework and Figure 11: Community Plan Bicycle & Pedestrian Framework of the
             South Cooper Mountain Community Plan. For properties in the Cooper Mountain Community Plan area,
             pedestrian, bicycle, and motor vehicle connections shall be provided between the on-site circulation system
             and adjacent existing and planned streets and trails as specified in Figures 6.4a and 6.2b of the Comprehensive
             Plan Transportation Element (Volume 1, Chapter 6). Deviations from South Cooper Mountain Community Plan
             Figure 10: Community Street Framework or Figure 11: Community Plan Bicycle & Pedestrian Framework or
             Cooper Mountain Community Plan Figure 6: Transportation Corridors shall be reviewed through a Planned
             Unit Development application. Streets and bicycle and pedestrian connections shall extend to the boundary of
             the parcel(s) under development and shall be designed to connect the proposed development’s streets,
           bicycle connections, and pedestrian connections to existing and future streets, bicycle connections, and
           pedestrian connections. [ORD 4531; April 2010] [ORD 4822; June 2022] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
2.   Loading areas, solid waste facilities and similar improvements.
     A.    For all uses in the MR zone and Commercial and Multiple Use zones, on-site service areas, outdoor storage
           areas, loading areas, waste storage, disposal facilities, and recycling containers shall not be located in between
           buildings and sidewalks on or along the primary street (as identified in Section 60.05.15.6.A.1). [ORD 4879;
           April 2026]
     B.    All on-site service areas, outdoor storage areas, waste storage, disposal facilities, recycling containers, and
           similar activities shall be located in an area not visible from a public street or shall be fully screened from view
           from a public street or Primary Accessway. [ORD 4531; April 2010] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
           1.   Screening be fully sight-obscuring, shall be constructed or grown a minimum of one 1 foot higher than
                the feature to be screened, and shall be accomplished by one or more of the following methods:
                a.    Solid screen wall constructed of wood, metal, masonry, or other exterior finish material utilized on
                     one or more buildings
                b.   A hedge with a minimum of 95 percent opacity within 2 years [ORD 2879; April 2026]
           2.   Loading docks and loading zones for manufacturing, assembly, fabricating, processing, packing, storage
                and wholesale and distribution activities which are the principal use of a building in Industrial districts,
                are exempt from this standard. [ORD 4857; October 2024] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     C.    Screening from public view by chain-link fence with or without slats is prohibited. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
3.   Pedestrian circulation.
     A.    Pedestrian connections shall be provided that link to adjacent existing and planned pedestrian facilities as
           specified in Tables 6.1 through 6.6 and Figures 6.1 through 6.23 of the Comprehensive Plan Transportation
           Element, and to the abutting public street system and on-site buildings, parking areas, and other facilities
           where pedestrian access is desired. For properties within the South Cooper Mountain Community Plan area,
           pedestrian connections shall be provided that link to adjacent existing and planned pedestrian facilities as
           specified in Figure 10: Community Plan Street Framework and Figure 11: Community Plan Bicycle & Pedestrian
           Framework of the South Cooper Mountain Community Plan and to the abutting public street system and on-
           site buildings, parking areas, and other facilities where pedestrian access is desired. For properties in the
           Cooper Mountain Community Plan area, pedestrian connections shall be provided that link to adjacent
           existing and planned pedestrian facilities as specified in Figure 6: Transportation Corridors of the Cooper
           Mountain Community Plan and to the abutting public street system, on-site buildings, parking areas, and other
           facilities where pedestrian access is desired. Deviations from South Cooper Mountain Community Plan Figure
           10: Community Street Framework or Figure 11: Community Plan Bicycle & Pedestrian Framework or Cooper
           Mountain Community Plan Figure 6: Transportation Corridors shall be reviewed through a Planned Unit
           Development application. Pedestrian connections shall be provided except when one or more of the following
           conditions exist: [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
           1.   Where physical or topographic conditions, such as a grade change of 10 feet or more at a property line to
                an adjacent pedestrian facility, make connections impractical, [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
           2.   Where uses including manufacturing, assembly, fabricating, processing, packing, storage and wholesale
                and distribution activities which are the principal use of a building in Industrial districts occur, [ORD 4857;
                October 2024]
           3.   Where on-site activities such as movement of trucks, forklifts, and other large equipment would present
                potential conflicts with pedestrians, or
           4.   Where buildings or other existing development on adjacent lands physically preclude a connection now
                or in the future.
     B.    A reasonably direct walkway connection is required between primary entrances, and public and private
           streets, transit stops, and other pedestrian destinations. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     C.    At least one reasonably direct pedestrian walkway into a site shall be provided for street frontages up to 300
           feet. An additional reasonably direct pedestrian walkway into a site shall be provided for every additional 300
           feet of street frontage. A reasonably direct walkway shall also be provided to any accessway abutting the site.
           This standard may be waived when topographic conditions, man-made features, natural areas, etc. preclude
           walkway extensions to adjacent properties. [ORD 4857; October 2024] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     D.    Pedestrian connections through parking lots shall be physically separated from adjacent vehicle parking and
           parallel vehicle traffic through the use of curbs, landscaping, trees, and lighting, if not otherwise provided in
           the parking lot design.
     E.    Where pedestrian connections cross driveways or vehicular access aisles a continuous walkway shall be
           provided and shall be composed of a different paving material than the primary on-site paving material.
     F.    Pedestrian walkways shall have a minimum of 5-foot wide unobstructed clearance and shall be paved with
           scored concrete or modular paving materials. In the event that the Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA)
           contains stricter standards for any pedestrian walkway, the ADA standards shall apply. [ORD 4531; April
           2010] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
4.   Street frontages and parking areas.
     A.    Surface parking areas abutting a public street shall provide perimeter parking lot landscaping which meets one
           of the following standards:
           1.   A minimum 6-foot wide planting strip between the right-of-way and the parking area. Pedestrian
                walkways and vehicular driveways may cross the planting strip. Trees shall be planted at a minimum
                2.5 inch caliper at a maximum of 30 feet on center. Planting strips shall be planted with an evergreen
                hedge that will provide a 30-inch-high screen and 50 percent opacity within two years. The maximum
                height shall be maintained at no more than 36 inches. Areas not covered by trees or hedge shall be
                landscaped with live ground cover. Bumper overhangs which intrude into the planting strip shall not
                impact required trees or hedge; or [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
           2.   A solid wall or fence 30 to 36 inches in height parallel to and not nearer than 4 feet from the right-of-way
                line. The area between the wall or fence and the street line shall be landscaped with live ground cover.
                Pedestrian walkways and vehicular driveways may cross the wall or fence. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
     B.    Ground-floor parking structures, parking garages, and tuck-under parking areas, inclusive of all parking spaces,
           drive aisles, and all vehicular maneuvering areas, within 20 feet of a public street shall provide screening which
           meets the following standards:
           1.   Portions of parking structures, parking garages, and tuck-under parking areas that are not occupied by
                ground-floor commercial space shall provide a minimum 5-foot building setback from all street-facing
                property lines.
           2.   The setback shall be landscaped, except for areas paved for vehicle and pedestrian access or land needed
                for above-ground utilities, as follows:
                a.   Evergreen shrubs a minimum 1 gallon in size planted next to each other to form a screen that is at
                     least 30 inches tall within 2 years. Additional shrubs in excess of those necessary to form a screen
                     are allowed. The shrubs shall be maintained at no more than 36 inches tall; and
                b.   Ground cover plants shall fully cover the remainder of the landscaped area.
           [ORD 4859; January 2025]​​
5.   Parking area landscaping.
     A.    Landscaped planter islands shall be required according to the following:
           1.   Residential uses in applicable Residential zones, one for every 8 contiguous parking spaces. [ORD 4859;
                January 2025]​
           2.   All uses in Commercial and Multiple Use zones, one for every 10 contiguous parking spaces. [ORD 4584;
                June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
           3.   Permitted non-residential uses and Conditional Uses in applicable Residential zones, one for every
                12 contiguous parking spaces. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
           4.   All uses in Employment / Industrial zones, one for every 12 contiguous parking spaces. [ORD 4584; June
                2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
     B.   The island shall have a minimum area of 70 square feet, and a minimum width of 6 feet, and shall be curbed to
          protect landscaping. The landscaped island shall be planted with a tree having a minimum mature height of 20
          feet. If a pole-mounted light is proposed to be installed within a landscaped planter island, and an applicant
          demonstrates that there is a physical conflict for siting the tree and the pole-mounted light together, the
          decision-making authority may waive the planting of the tree, provided that at least 75 percent of the required
          islands contain trees. Landscaped planter islands shall be evenly spaced throughout the parking area. [ORD
          4859; January 2025]​
     C.   Linear raised walkways within the parking area, connecting the parking spaces and on-site building(s), may be
          counted towards the total required number of landscaped islands, provided that all of the following is met:
          [ORD 4876; January 2026]
          1.   Trees are spaced a maximum of 30 feet on center on a minimum of one side of the walkway. [ORD 4876;
               January 2026]
          2.   The minimum unobstructed walkway width is five feet. [ORD 4876; January 2026]
          3.   The walkway is separated from the parking area by curbs, bollards, or other means on both sides. [ORD
               4876; January 2026]
          4.   Trees are located in planting area with groundcover or planted in covered tree wells.
          5.   Trees within the linear walkway area shall constitute no more than 50 percent of the total required
               number of trees within required landscaped planter islands. All remaining required trees shall be located
               within landscaped planter islands. [ORD 4876; January 2026]
          [ORD 4531; April 2010]
     D.   Trees planted within required landscaped planter islands or the linear walkway shall be of a type and species
          identified by the City of Beaverton Tree List or an alternative approved by the City Arborist. [ORD 4859;
          January 2025]​[ORD 4876; January 2026]
     E.   A new development that adds more than one-half acre of new surface parking (newly constructed parking
          and/or paved parking area that was removed and replaced) to a lot shall provide trees and walkways along
          driveways or a minimum of 30 percent Tree Canopy coverage over the additional parking lot area. Lots
          meeting Section 60.30.15.10.c are exempt from this requirement. Tree Canopy coverage shall be calculated
          according to Section 60.30.15.10.c. For the purposes of this standard, surface parking shall include the
          perimeter of all parking spaces including maneuvering areas and interior parking lot landscaping. For the
          length of the driveway or driveways, excluding segments where access to drive aisles, loading area access,
          loading berths, or other vehicle maneuvering areas intersect with or otherwise interrupt the driveway's
          planting/pedestrian area, the following shall be provided: [ORD 4859; January 2025]​ [ORD 4876; January
          2026]
          1.   One tree from the City of Beaverton Tree List with an expected Tree Canopy of at least 15 feet for every
               25 feet of driveway. The expected Tree Canopy measurement shall use the anticipated Tree Canopy of the
               proposed tree at maturity but no more than 15 years after planting. For driveway segments of sufficient
               length to require more than one tree, the trees shall be planted in a continuous shared planter strip
               unless site conditions involving drive aisles, pedestrian walkways, or utilities shown in the application
               interrupt the landscape planter strip. The trees' expected canopies at maturity but no more than 15 years
               after planting shall be contiguous (with gaps of less than 3 feet between expected canopies). Trees
               planted to meet this standard shall be planted and maintained consistent with 2023 ANSI A300
               standards. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
          2.   Pedestrian walkways along at least one side of the driveway designed consistent with Section
               60.05.20.3.D through F.
          [ORD 4844; August 2023]
6.   Off-Street parking. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​[ORD 4879; April 2026]
     A.   For all uses in the MR zone and Commercial and Multiple Use zones, vehicle areas including off-street surface
          parking areas shall not be located between a building and an abutting Primary Frontage.
             1.    Auto parking areas shall be behind the maximum setback or behind a line drawn parallel to the street at
                   the point where the building is closest to the street, whichever is closest to the street. In no
                   circumstances shall the parking area be closer than 20 feet to the right-of-way.
             2.    For sites with multiple frontages, auto parking areas shall be no closer than 20 feet to the right-of-way on
                   non-primary frontages, regardless of building location. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
7.    Walkways along streets and primary building elevations in Commercial and Multiple Use zones. [ORD 4584; June
      2012] [ORD 4876; January 2026]
      A.     A walkway internal to the site is required along building elevations that include a primary building entrance,
             multiple tenant entrances or display windows. The walkway shall be a minimum of 10 feet wide, and provide
             an unobstructed path at least 5 feet wide at building entrances, and along elevations containing display
             windows. Walkways shall be paved with scored concrete or modular paving materials. If adjacent to parking
             areas, the walkway shall be separated from the parking by a raised curb. This standard is not intended to be
             cumulative for buildings located at the property line, or setback less than 10 feet to the property line, and are
             adjacent to sidewalks in the public right-of-way that are a minimum of 10 feet wide and provide an
             unobstructed path at least 5 feet wide at building entrances and along elevations containing display
             windows. [ORD 4531; April 2010] [ORD 4857; October 2024] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​ [ORD 4876; January
             2026]
      B.     Residential development fronting common greens or shared courts and detached units fronting streets are
             exempt from the standards of 7.B above and are subject to the Engineering Design Manual. [ORD 4542; June
             2010] [ORD 4576; January 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​[ORD 4879; April 2026]
8.    Connect on-site buildings, parking, and other improvements with identifiable streets and drive aisles in
      Commercial, Multiple Use, and applicable Residential zones. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
      A.     Parking lot drive aisles that link public streets and/or private streets with parking stalls shall be designed as
             private streets consistent with the standard as described under Section 60.05.20.8.B., unless one of the
             following is met:
             1.    The parking lot drive aisle is less than 100 feet long;
             2.    The parking lot drive aisle serves 2 or less residential units; or
             3.    The parking lot drive aisle provides direct access to angled or perpendicular parking stalls.
      B.     Private streets, common greens, and shared courts shall meet the following standards:
             1.    Private streets serving non-residential uses and residential uses having five or more units shall have raised
                   curbs and minimum 5-foot wide unobstructed sidewalks on both sides. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
             2.    Private streets serving less than 5 residential units shall have raised curbs and a minimum 5-foot wide
                   unobstructed sidewalk on at least one side. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
             3.    When common greens and shared courts are utilized, an unobstructed walkway a minimum of 5 feet
                   wide shall be provided within the common green or shared court. [ORD 4542; June 2010] [ORD 4859;
                   January 2025]​
             [ORD 4531; April 2010]
9.    Ground floor uses in parking structures.
      A.     Parking structures located on Neighborhood Route, Collector, Arterial, or Principal Arterial streets and on
             primary public street frontages in the CM-CS zoning district shall incorporate one or more active retail or
             commercial uses other than parking at ground level along the entire portion of the structure fronting onto
             such routes. Compliance to this standard is not required when a semi-subterranean parking structure is
             proposed, provided that the height of such structures, or portions thereof, is not greater than 3.5 feet above
             the elevation of the adjoining walkway or sidewalk. [ORD 4859; January 2025]​[ORD 4879; April 2026]
[ORD 4332, 01/01/2005; ORD 4531, 04/01/2010; ORD 4542, 06/17/2010; ORD 4576, 01/06/2012; ORD 4584,
06/01/2012; ORD 4822, 06/30/2022; ORD 4844, 08/18/2023; ORD 4857, 10/03/2024; ORD 4859, 01/02/2025; ORD 4876,
01/20/2026; ORD 4879, 04/02/2026]
Effective on: 4/2/2026
60.05.25. Landscape, Open Space, and Natural Areas Design Standards.
All standards apply in all zoning districts unless otherwise noted in Section 60.05.11 or within the standards below. [ORD
4859; January 2025]​
1.   Minimum landscape requirements for residential developments consisting of 2 or 3 units of Attached Housing or
     Compact Detached Housing. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
     A.    All areas of the lot not occupied by structures or pavement shall be landscaped as defined in Section
           60.05.25.3.B.5 and Section 60.05.25.4. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
     [ORD 4515; September 2009]
2.   Minimum Open Space and landscape requirements for residential developments consisting of 4 to 7 units of
     Attached Housing or Compact Detached Housing. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​
     A.    For Attached Dwellings, a minimum of 15 percent of the gross site area shall be Open Space and landscaped as
           defined in Section 60.05.25.3.B.5 and Section 60.05.25.4. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4857; October
           2024] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​[ORD 4879; April 2026]
     B.    For Compact Detached Housing, an attached private patio or yard area of at least 300 square feet in size shall
           be provided. No dimension of private open space area shall be less than 10 feet. [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD
           4859; January 2025]​
     [ORD 4515; September 2009] [ORD 4857; October 2024]
3.   Minimum Open Space and landscape requirements for residential developments consisting of 8 or more units of
     Attached Housing or Compact Detached Housing; non-residential developments; and Mixed Use developments.
     [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4859; January 2025]​[ORD 4879; April 2026]
     A.    Usable Open Space shall be provided consistent with Table 60.05.25.3.A and the following: [ORD 4857;
           October 2024] [ORD 4879; April 2026]
           1.   For any building, each square foot of either Publicly Accessible Open Space (PAOS) or Shared Open Space
                may count as 1.33 square feet toward the Usable Open Space requirement. In no case shall both a PAOS
                and a Shared Open Space count as 1.33 square feet toward the requirement.
           2.   Shared Open Space and Publicly Accessible Open Space shall only count toward the Usable Open Space
                required for a residential-only or Mixed Use building with residential units if the Shared Open Space or
                Publicly Accessible Open Space is within 300 feet of that building.
           3.   Shared Open Space and Publicly Accessible Open Space shall only count toward the Usable Open Space
                required for non-residential buildings or Mixed Use buildings with zero residential units if it is within 600
                feet of that building.
           4.   A Common Community Room shall only count toward a maximum of 20 percent of the Usable Open
                Space Requirement.
           5.   When any portion of an exterior Usable Open Space is within 20 feet of any right of way with a Collector
                or higher functional classification, the Usable Open Space shall be separated from the street by a
                constructed barrier at least 30 inches in height. A constructed barrier could include wall segments,
                bollards, fencing, or other constructed elements that prevent vehicular access to the Usable Open Space.
                Obstacles installed may be designed to be removable to provide vehicular access for activities such as
                maintenance.
           6.   Compact Detached Housing Common Green and Compact Detached Housing Shared Court shall only be
                used in Compact Detached Housing developments.
           7.   A Compact Detached Housing Shared Court shall only count toward a maximum of 25 percent of the
                Usable Open Space requirement.
           8.   Vehicular circulation areas and parking areas, unless provided as part of a Common Green or Shared
                Court, shall not be considered required Usable Open Space. [ORD 4879; April 2026]

   Table 60.05.25.3.A Table Notes
     1. “Site” shall mean the portion of the site affected by the application, including any area affected by grading;
        development of buildings and structures; pavement removal or installation; or open space or landscaping
        removal or installation.
     2. Near Frequent Transit Service shall mean that any portion of the site is within one-half mile of the centerline
        of streets with Frequent Transit Service or the center of tracks with Frequent Transit Service.


   B.   Site landscaping shall be provided as follows. For the purposes of this sub-section, “site” shall mean the portion
        of the site affected by the application, including any area affected by grading; development of buildings and
        structures; pavement removal or installation; or open space or landscaping removal or installation.
          1.    Landscaped areas with live plantings shall be provided as follows:
                a.      For sites one-half acre and larger:
                        i.    Commercial, Employment/Industrial, and Residential districts: A minimum of 10 percent of site
                              area
                        ii.   ​Multiple Use Districts: A minimum of 5 percent of the site area.
                b.      For sites smaller than one-half acre: No landscape area required unless specified in other standards.
                c.      For sites 1 acre or larger, at least 80 percent of the landscape area shall be within 150 feet of the
                        building footprints of buildings proposed for development or expansion as part of the development
                        application.
          2.    Up to 50 percent of the landscaping required by Section 60.05.25.3.B may be met by areas with live
                plantings provided to satisfy the requirements of Section 60.05.25.3.A. Landscaping with plantings that are
                provided to meet other requirements of this code, including but not limited to screening requirements,
                buffering requirements, parking lot island requirements, setback design requirements, and Natural Area
                requirements, may be used to meet up to 100 percent of the landscaping required by this provision.
          3.    For the purposes of this Section, vehicular circulation areas and parking areas, unless provided as part of a
                Common Green or Shared Court, shall not be considered required landscaped area.
          4.    The following shall be planted or preserved within the required landscape area:
                a.      Trees on sites one-half acre and larger: One tree for every 3,000 square feet of total site area.
                b.      Trees on sites less than one-half: One tree per 1,000 square feet of total site area not paved or
                        occupied by a building.
                c.      Trees required in a. and b. above shall be deciduous or evergreen trees that are able to reach a height
                        of 20 feet and a canopy width of 20 feet at maturity.
                d.      One shrub for every 20 square feet of required landscape area.
                e.      Preserved trees or shrubs shall meet the standards in Section 60.05.25.4 to count toward the
                        landscaping requirements in this section.
                f.      Live ground cover shall be provided in the portion of the required landscape area not covered by trees
                        or shrubs. Bare gravel, rock, bark or other similar materials may be used but shall not be a substitute
                        for ground cover plantings and shall be limited to no more than 25 percent of the required landscape
                        area.
          5.    All site areas not planted with trees, shrubs or other vegetated landscaping and also not occupied by
                structures, hardscaped areas (including paved areas), and sensitive natural areas shall be planted with live
                ground cover plants or other plants as well as turf grasses. Mulch, as a ground cover, shall be composed of
                a naturally occurring material, have a natural color, and confined to areas underneath plants and within
                areas expected to be underneath plants at maturity. Mulch is not a substitute for ground cover plants.
                [ORD 4879; April 2026]
4.   General Landscaping Requirements. The following standards apply to new landscaping required by Section
     60.05.25:
     A.    New plantings shall be selected from the following approved plant lists:
           1.        City of Beaverton Tree List
           2.        Clean Water Services’ Design and Construction Standards, Appendix A (2019)
           3.        Metro Native Plants Booklet (2018)
           4.        Portland Plant List (2016)
     B.    Irrigation shall be provided to ensure all site plantings will survive their establishment period. Establishment
           period irrigation shall be provided through one of the following options or a combination of options:
           1.        A permanent, in-ground irrigation system with an automatic controller.
           2.        An irrigation system designed and certified by a licensed landscape architect as part of a landscape plan
                     that provides sufficient water to ensure that the plants will become established. The system does not
               have to be permanent if a licensed landscape architect certifies that the plants chosen can be adequately
               served by the proposed irrigation system.
          3.   Irrigation by hand for a maximum of 500 square feet per site.
     C.   Landscaping shall be planted at sizes no less than the following (measures shall be taken based on the
          American Standard for Nursery Stock ANSI standards). In the case of a code conflict, the higher requirement
          shall be met.
          1.   Deciduous canopy trees shall be a minimum of 1.5-inch caliper size, balled and burlapped; and
          2.   Deciduous ornamental trees shall be a minimum of 1.5-inch caliper size, balled and burlapped; and
          3.   Evergreen trees shall be a minimum of 8 feet in height, balled and burlapped; and
          4.   Evergreen and deciduous shrubs shall be a minimum of 24 inches high from finished grade and a
               minimum of 1 gallon in size, except dwarf shrubs such as boxwood, which have no minimum size; and
          5.   Ferns and perennials shall be at least 1 gallon in size; and
          6.   Ground-covers plants including ornamental grasses shall be at least 4-inch pot size.
          7.   Areas subject to Clean Water Services regulations including stormwater facilities, vegetated corridors, and
               sensitive natural areas shall be planted consistent with Clean Water Services requirements. [ORD 4879;
               April 2026]
     D.   Plant diversity:
          1.   If more than 17 trees are provided on a site, no more than 33 percent of the trees can be of one species;
               and
          2.   If more than 25 shrubs are provided on a site, no more than 75 percent can be of one species.
     E.   A minimum of 25 percent of landscape plantings provided on a site shall be drought-tolerant species as
          identified on an approved plant list.
     F.   A minimum of 50 percent of trees and 50 percent of shrubs provided on a site shall be native species, as
          identified in one of the plant lists in Section 60.05.25.4.A.
     [ORD 4859; January 2025]
5.   Usable Open Space. The following standards apply to the Usable Open Space types in Table 60.05.25.3.A. Additional
     standards are found in Section 60.05.25.6 Common Greens and Section 60.05.25.7 Shared Courts. [ORD 4879; April
     2026]

                                    Figure 60.05.25.5: Usable Open Space Types




Image Text:
A - Publicly Accessible Open Space (PAOS)
B - Shared Open Space
C - Common community room opening into Shared Open Space
D - Private open space (e.g. balconies, roof, etc.)
A.   Publicly Accessible Open Spaces (PAOS) shall be designed to create usable open space for public use. PAOS
     shall:
     1.   Be large enough to fit a 20-foot by 20-foot square inside of it; and
     2.   If located between a building and public sidewalk, be bordered on two sides by building facades with
          some combination of commercial uses, primary residential entrances or primary office entrances with at
          least one door and windows facing the PAOS and providing the ability to view the PAOS from inside the
          building; and
     3.   Provide at least 60 percent of the total PAOS area as open to the sky free of permanent weather
          protection; and
     4.   Include at least one bench or ledge at seating height per 200 square feet that can seat two people side by
          side; and
     5.   Include landscaping on at least 20 percent of its area. Spaces 800 square feet or larger shall provide one
          tree per 800 square feet of open space; and
     6.   Be directly accessible from a public right of way; and
     7.   Be publicly accessible for a minimum of 12 consecutive hours per day. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
B.   Shared Open Spaces, including but not limited to courtyards, playgrounds, community gardens, patios,
     rooftop open spaces, and terraces, shall:
     1.   Be large enough to fit a 20-foot by 20-foot square inside of it if enclosed on three sides or fewer and be
          large enough to fit a 40-foot by 40-foot square inside of it if enclosed on four sides. If enclosed on all four
          sides, the space does not qualify as a Shared Open Space if all walls bordering the open space have
          abuilding height more than 1.5 times the Shared Open Space perpendicular to that wall; and
     2.   Provide at least 60 percent of the total Shared Open Space area as open to the sky free of permanent
          weather protection; and
     3.   Include at least one bench or ledge at seating height per 200 square feet that can seat two people side by
          side; and
     4.   Include landscaping on at least 20 percent of its area. Spaces at grade that are 500 square feet or larger
          shall provide one tree per 500 square feet of open space.
     5.   In addition, include at least one of the following elements:
          a.   A children's play structure, play area, or nature play area that incorporates at least 100 square feet
               of shade at 3 p.m. June 21, precipitation protection for a minimum contiguous footprint of 100
               square feet, or both;
          b.   Fenced and gated dog run or dog park;
          c.   An outdoor activity component or components:
               i.     sport (examples: basketball, futsal, tennis, volleyball, skating); or
               ii.    game area (examples: bocce court, horseshoe court); or
               iii.   water activity (examples: hot tub, swimming pool, water play); or
               iv.    Food (examples: Picnic lawn, outdoor cooking/dining, grill area);
          d.   If the property is within Cooper Mountain, a plaza large enough to fit a 20-foot by 20-foot square
               inside that is consistent with the requirements of Section 60.05.25.16.B that incorporates at least
               100 square feet of shade at 3 p.m. June 21, precipitation protection for a minimum contiguous
               footprint of 100 square feet, or both;
          e.   Plaza large enough to fit a 20-foot by 20-foot square inside that is consistent with the requirements
               of a Publicly Accessible Open space, except for Section 60.05.25.5.2, 6 and 7, that incorporates at
               least 100 square feet of shade at 3 p.m. June 21, precipitation protection for a minimum contiguous
               footprint of 100 square feet, or both;
          f.   Gardening area with at least 100 square feet of planting area; or [ORD 4879; April 2026]
C.   Common Community Rooms shall be accessible to building occupants and designed to serve as gathering
     places. Common Community Rooms may include lounges, fitness rooms, shared kitchens, dining areas, co-
          working spaces, game rooms, or other spaces that provide opportunities for shared experiences. Common
          Community Rooms shall meet the following standards:
          1.   Common Community Rooms shall be large enough so a 15-foot by 15-foot square will fit inside it; and
          2.   The Common Community Room shall have a minimum floor-to-floor height of 11 feet; and
          3.   The Common Community Room shall have one wall along an exterior facade of the building and shall
               have at least 30 percent glazing measured from the interior: or
          4.   Common Community Rooms shall have direct access to a PAOS or a shared open space that is large
               enough to fit a 20-foot by 20-foot square inside of and that is at least 60 percent open to the sky. [ORD
               4879; April 2026]
     D.   Private Open Spaces shall meet the following design standards:
          1.   Shall be attached to and directly accessible from an individual residential unit; and
          2.   Shall be large enough to fit a 5-foot by 6-foot rectangle inside of it; and
          3.   Shall be screened a minimum 50 percent from abutting units to provide privacy; and
          4.   Shall have a minimum clear height dimension of 8 feet 6 inches. [ORD 4879; April 2026]
6.   Common Greens. Common greens are intended to serve as a common open space amenity for residents. The
     following standards apply to common greens for compact detached housing: [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4782;
     April 2020]
     A.   General.
          1.   The common green shall be placed in a tract and shall provide access for pedestrians and bicycles. [ORD
               4782; April 2020]
          2.   The minimum dimension of a common green is 15 feet and must include a 5-foot-wide walkway. The size
               of the common green right-of-way must be sufficient to accommodate expected users and uses. The size
               must take into consideration the characteristics of the site and vicinity, such as the pedestrian system,
               structures, natural features, and the community activities that may occur within the common
               green. [ORD 4859; January 2025]
          3.   Common greens may be dead-end or extend between streets. If a public pedestrian connection is
               provided, the pedestrian connection should either directly abut or pass through the common green, or be
               in close proximity. See Figure 1. Common greens may also have frontage on more than one intersecting
               street, if the green is located at the corner of the intersecting streets. See Figure 2.
          4.   Where a public pedestrian connection abuts or passes through a common green, the public pedestrian
               connection must include design features that distinguish the pedestrian connection from the common
               green, such as perimeter landscaping, low decorative fencing, or paving materials.
          5.   Parking for dwellings fronting a common green shall be accessed from an alley or access easement.

                                        Blocks with Through Common Green




                                                       Figure 2
                                                Corner Common Green




[ORD 4542; June 2010]
7.   Shared Courts for Compact Detached Housing. Shared courts are intended to serve pedestrians and vehicles within
     the same circulation area, while ensuring that all can use the area safely. See Figure 3. Special paving and other
     street elements should be designed to encourage slow vehicle speeds and to signify the shared court’s intended use
     by pedestrians as well as vehicles. See Figure 4. Access from a shared court is limited to ensure low traffic volumes
     that can allow a safe mixing of pedestrians and vehicles. Shared courts are limited to zones intended for more
     intense development to facilitate efficient use of land while preserving the landscape-intensive character of lower-
     density zones. The following standards apply to shared courts: [ORD 4584; June 2012] [ORD 4782; April 2020]
     A.    General.
           1.   The minimum width of a shared court right-of-way is 20 feet. The size of the shared court right-of-way
                must be sufficient to accommodate expected users and uses. The size must take into consideration the
          characteristics of the site and vicinity, such as the pedestrian system, structures, traffic safety, natural
          features, and the community activities that may occur within the shared court.
     2.   A shared court may be up to 150 feet long. The Facilities Review Committee may allow longer shared
          courts, subject to additional conditions of approval or design requirements. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
     3.   Shared courts shall not allow through movement of vehicles to two or more streets unless specifically
          allowed by the Facilities Review Committee, which may impose additional conditions of approval or
          design requirements. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
     4.   No more than 16 lots may have a front lot line on a shared court. [ORD 4782; April 2020]
B.   Shared Court paving design. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
     1.   Vehicle maneuvering, parking and emergency access areas within shared courts shall be constructed of
          brick pavers, stone, or scored or colored concrete. Asphalt is permitted within a shared court, but shall
          not exceed 25 percent of the area of a shared court.
     2.   Varied paving materials and colors, bollards, fences, landscaping, lighting and other street furnishings
          shall be used to differentiate vehicle activities within shared courts from other activities such as
          pedestrian areas, bicycle areas, play areas, gardens, etc.
     3.   Driveway widths for the first 20 feet where a shared court meets a public street are: [ORD 4859; January
          2025]
          a.   Ten feet where a driveway provides access to 10 or fewer units on a Local Street and
          b.   Twenty feet where a driveway provides access to more than 10 units or when access is on a Collector
               or Arterial street. [ORD 4584; June 2012]
